IN\n   354→        const floating[::1] sample_weight,             # IN\n   355→        const floating[:, ::1] centers_old,            # IN\n   356→        const floating[:, ::1] center_half_distances,  # OUT\n   363→        bint update_centers) noexcept nogil:\n   364→    \"\"\"K-means combined EM step for one dense data chunk.\n   365→\n   366→    Compute the partial contribution of a single data chunk to the labels and\n   367→    centers.\n   368→    \"\"\"\n   369→    cdef:\n   370→        int n_samples = labels.shape[0]\n   371→        int n_clusters = centers_old.shape[0]\n   372→        int n_features = centers_old.shape[1]\n   373→\n   374→        floating upper_bound, distance\n   375→        int i, j, k, label\n   376→\n   377→    for i in range(n_samples):\n   378→        upper_bound = upper_bounds[i]\n   379→        bounds_tight = 0\n   380→        label = labels[i]\n   381→\n   382→        # OUT\n        floating[:, ::1] lower_bounds,                 # OUT\n        int n_threads):\n    \"\"\"Initialize upper and lower bounds for each sample for dense input data.\n\n    Given X, centers and the pairwise distances divided by 2.0 between the\n    centers this calculates the upper bounds and lower bounds for each sample.\n    The upper bound for each sample is set to the distance between the sample\n    and the closest center.\n\n    The lower bound for each sample is a one-dimensional array of n_clusters.\n    For each sample i assume that the previously assigned cluster is c1 and the\n    previous closest distance is dist, for a new cluster c2, the\n    lower_bound[i][c2] is set to distance between the sample and this new\n    cluster, if and only if dist > center_half_distances[c1][c2]. This prevents\n    computation of unnecessary distances for each sample to the clusters that\n    it is unlikely to be assigned to.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    X : ndarray of shape (n_samples, n_features), dtype=floating\n        The input data.\n\n    centers : ndarray of shape (n_clusters, n_features), dtype=floating\n        The cluster centers.\n\n    center_half_distances : ndarray of shape (n_clusters, n_clusters), \\\n            dtype=floating\n        The half of the distance between any 2 clusters centers.\n\n    labels : ndarray of shape(n_samples), dtype=int\n        The label for each sample. This array is modified in place.\n\n    upper_bounds : ndarray of shape(n_samples,), dtype=floating\n        The upper bound on the distance between each sample and its closest\n        cluster center. This array is modified in place.\n\n    lower_bounds : ndarray, of shape(n_samples, n_clusters), dtype=floating\n        The lower bound on the distance between each sample and each cluster\n        center. OUT\n        floating[:, ::1] lower_bounds,                 # OUT\n        int n_threads):\n    \"\"\"Initialize upper and lower bounds for each sample for sparse input data.\n\n    Given X, centers and the pairwise distances divided by 2.0 between the\n    centers this calculates the upper bounds and lower bounds for each sample.\n    The upper bound for each sample is set to the distance between the sample\n    and the closest center.\n\n    The lower bound for each sample is a one-dimensional array of n_clusters.\n    For each sample i assume that the previously assigned cluster is c1 and the\n    previous closest distance is dist, for a new cluster c2, the\n    lower_bound[i][c2] is set to distance between the sample and this new\n    cluster, if and only if dist > center_half_distances[c1][c2]. For a\n   211→        given index and center, X[index] = center.\n   212→    \"\"\"\n   213→    n_samples, n_features = X.shape\n   214→\n   215→    centers = np.empty((n_clusters, n_features), dtype=X.dtype)\n   216→\n   217→    # Set the number of local seeding trials if none is given\n   218→    if n_local_trials is None:\n   219→        # This is what Arthur/Vassilvitskii tried, but did not report\n   220→        # specific results for other than mentioning in the conclusion\n   221→        # that it helped.\n   222→        n_local_trials = 2 + int(np.log(n_clusters))\n   223→\n   224→    # Pick first center randomly and track index of point\n   225→    center_id = random_state.choice(n_samples, p=sample_weight / sample_weight.sum())\n   226→    indices = np.full(n_clusters, -1, dtype=int)\n   227→    if sp.issparse(X):\n   228→        centers[0] = X[[center_id]].toarray()\n   229→    else:\n   230→        centers[0] = X[center_id]\n   231→    indices[0] = center_id\n   232→\n   233→    # Initialize list of closest distances and calculate current potential\n   234→    closest_dist_sq = _euclidean_distances(\n   235→        centers[0, np.newaxis], X, Y_norm_squared=x_squared_norms, squared=True\n   236→    )\n   237→    current_pot = closest_dist_sq @ sample_weight\n   238→\n   239→    # Pick the remaining n_clusters-1 points\n   240→    for c in range(1, n_clusters):\n   241→        # Choose center candidates by sampling with probability proportional\n   242→        # to the squared distance to the closest existing center\n   243→        rand_vals = random_state.uniform(size=n_local_trials) * current_pot\n   244→        candidate_ids = np.searchsorted(\n   245→            stable_cumsum(sample_weight * closest_dist_sq), rand_vals\n   246→        )\n   247→        # XXX: numerical imprecision can result in a candidate_id out of range\n   248→        np.clip(candidate_ids, None, closest_dist_sq.size - 1, out=candidate_ids)\n   249→\n   250→        # Compute distances to center candidates\n   251→        distance_to_candidates = _euclidean_distances(\n   252→            X[candidate_ids], X, Y_norm_squared=x_squared_norms, squared=True\n   253→        )\n   254→\n   255→        # update closest distances squared and potential for each candidate\n   256→        np.minimum(closest_dist_sq, distance_to_candidates, out=distance_to_candidates)\n   257→        candidates_pot = distance_to_candidates @ sample_weight.reshape(-1, 1)\n   258→\n   259→        # Decide which candidate is the best\n   260→        best_candidate = np.argmin(candidates_pot)\n   261→        current_pot = candidates_pot[best_candidate]\n   262→        closest_dist_sq = distance_to_candidates[best_candidate]\n   263→        best_candidate = candidate_ids[best_candidate]\n   264→\n   265→        # Permanently add best center candidate found in local tries\n   266→        if sp.issparse(X):\n   267→            centers[c] = X[[best_candidate]].toarray()\n   268→        else:\n   269→            centers[c] = X[best_candidate]\n   270→        indices[c] = best_candidate\n   271→\n   272→    return centers, indices\n   273→\n   274→\n   275→###############################################################################\n   276→# K-means batch estimation by EM (expectation maximization)\n   277→\n   278→\n   279→def _tolerance(X, tol):\n   280→    \"\"\"Return a tolerance which is dependent on the dataset.\"\"\"\n   281→    if tol == 0:\n   282→        return 0\n   283→    if sp.issparse(X):\n   284→        variances = mean_variance_axis(X, axis=0)[1]\n   285→    else:\n   286→        variances = np.var(X, axis=0)\n   287→    return np.mean(variances) * tol\n   288→\n   289→\n   290→@validate_params(\n   291→    {\n   292→        \"X\": [\"array-like\", \"sparse matrix\"],\n   293→        \"sample_weight\": [\"array-like\", None],\n   294→        \"return_n_iter\": [bool],\n   295→    },\n   296→    prefer_skip_nested_validation=False,\n   297→)\n   298→def k_means(\n   299→    X,\n   300→    n_clusters,\n   301→    *,\n   302→    sample_weight=None,\n   303→    init=\"k-means++\",\n   304→    n_init=\"auto\",\n   305→    max_iter=300,\n   306→    verbose=False,\n   307→    tol=1e-4,\n   308→    random_state=None,\n   309→    copy_x=True,\n   310→    algorithm=\"lloyd\",\n   311→    return_n_iter=False,\n   312→):\n   313→    \"\"\"Perform K-means clustering algorithm.\n   314→\n   315→    Read more in the :ref:`User Guide <k_means>`.\n   316→\n   317→    Parameters\n   318→    ----------\n   319→    X : {array-like, sparse matrix} of shape (n_samples, n_features)\n   320→        The observations to cluster. It must be noted that the data\n   321→        will be converted to C ordering, which will cause a memory copy\n   322→        if the given data is not C-contiguous.\n   323→\n   324→    n_clusters : int\n   325→        The number of clusters to form as well as the number of\n   326→        centroids to generate.\n   327→\n   328→    sample_weight : array-like of shape (n_samples,), default=None\n   329→        The weights for each observation in `X`. If `None`, all observations\n   330→        are assigned equal weight. `sample_weight` is not used during\n   331→        initialization if `init` is a callable or a user provided array.\n   332→\n   333→    init : {'k-means++', 'random'}, callable or array-like of shape \\\n   334→            (n_clusters, n_features), default='k-means++'\n   335→        Method for initialization:\n   336→\n   337→        - `'k-means++'` : selects initial cluster centers for k-mean\n   338→          clustering in a smart way to speed up convergence. See section\n   339→          Notes in k_init for more details.\n   340→        - `'random'`: choose `n_clusters` observations (rows) at random from data\n   341→          for the initial centroids.\n   342→        - If an array is passed, it should be of shape `(n_clusters, n_features)`\n   343→          and gives the initial centers.\n   344→        - If a callable is passed, it should take arguments `X`, `n_clusters` and a\n   345→          random state and return an initialization.\n   346→\n   347→    n_init : 'auto' or int, default=\"auto\"\n   348→        Number of time the k-means algorithm will be run with different\n   349→        centroid seeds. The final results will be the best output of\n   350→        n_init consecutive runs in terms of inertia.\n   351→\n   352→        When `n_init='auto'`, the number of runs depends on the value of init:\n   353→        10 if using `init='random'` or `init` is a callable;\n   354→        1 if using `init='k-means++'` or `init` is an array-like.\n   355→\n   356→        .. versionadded:: 1.2\n   357→           Added 'auto' option for `n_init`.\n   358→\n   359→        .. versionchanged:: 1.4\n   360→           Default value for `n_init` changed to `'auto'`.\n   361→\n   362→    max_iter : int, default=300\n   363→        Maximum number of iterations of the k-means algorithm to run.\n   364→\n   365→    verbose : bool, default=False\n   366→        Verbosity mode.\n   367→\n   368→    tol : float, default=1e-4\n   369→        Relative tolerance with regards to Frobenius norm of the difference\n   370→        in the cluster centers of two consecutive iterations to declare\n   371→        convergence.\n   372→\n   373→    random_state : int, RandomState instance or None, default=None\n   374→        Determines random number generation for centroid initialization. Use\n   375→        an int to make the randomness deterministic.\n   376→        See :term:`Glossary <random_state>`.\n   377→\n   378→    copy_x : bool, default=True\n   379→        When pre-computing distances it is more numerically accurate to center\n   380→        the data first. If `copy_x` is True (default), then the original data is\n   381→        not modified. If False, the original data is modified, and put back\n   382→        before the function returns, but small numerical differences may be\n   383→        introduced by subtracting and then adding the data mean. Note that if\n   384→        the original data is not C-contiguous, a copy will be made even if\n   385→        `copy_x` is False. If the original data is sparse, but not in CSR format,\n   386→        a copy will be made even if `copy_x` is False.\n   387→\n   388→    algorithm : {\"lloyd\", \"elkan\"}, default=\"lloyd\"\n   389→        K-means algorithm to use. The classical EM-style algorithm is `\"lloyd\"`.\n   390→        The `\"elkan\"` variation can be more efficient on some datasets with\n   391→        well-defined clusters, by using the triangle inequality. However it's\n   392→        more memory intensive due to the allocation of an extra array of shape\n   393→        `(n_samples, n_clusters)`.\n   394→\n   395→        .. versionchanged:: 0.18\n   396→            Added Elkan algorithm\n   397→\n   398→        .. versionchanged:: 1.1\n   399→            Renamed \"full\" to \"lloyd\", and deprecated \"auto\" and \"full\".\n   400→            Changed \"auto\" to use \"lloyd\" instead of \"elkan\".\n   401→\n   402→    return_n_iter : bool, default=False\n   403→        Whether or not to return the number of iterations.\n   404→\n   405→    Returns\n   406→    -------\n   407→    centroid : ndarray of shape (n_clusters, n_features)\n   408→        Centroids found at the last iteration of k-means.\n   409→\n   410→    label : ndarray of shape (n_samples,)\n   411→        The `label[i]` is the code or index of the centroid the\n   412→        i'th observation is closest to.\n   413→\n   414→    inertia : float\n   415→        The final value of the inertia criterion (sum of squared distances to\n   416→        the closest centroid for all observations in the training set).\n   417→\n   418→    best_n_iter : int\n   419→        Number of iterations corresponding to the best results.\n   420→        Returned only if `return_n_iter` is set to True.\n   421→\n   422→    Examples\n   423→    --------\n   424→    >>> import numpy as np\n   425→    >>> from sklearn.cluster import k_means\n   426→    >>> X = np.array([[1, 2], [1, 4], [1, 0],\n   427→    ...               [10, 2], [10, 4], [10, 0]])\n   428→    >>> centroid, label, inertia = k_means(\n   429→    ...     X, n_clusters=2, n_init=\"auto\", random_state=0\n   430→    ... )\n   431→    >>> centroid\n   432→    array([[10.,  2.],\n   433→           [ 1.,  2.]])\n   434→    >>> label\n   435→    array([1, 1, 1, 0, 0, 0], dtype=int32)\n   436→    >>> inertia\n   437→    16.0\n   438→    \"\"\"\n   439→    est = KMeans(\n   440→        n_clusters=n_clusters,\n   441→        init=init,\n   442→        n_init=n_init,\n   443→        max_iter=max_iter,\n   444→        verbose=verbose,\n   445→        tol=tol,\n   446→        random_state=random_state,\n   447→        copy_x=copy_x,\n   448→        algorithm=algorithm,\n   449→    ).fit(X, sample_weight=sample_weight)\n   450→    if return_n_iter:\n   451→        return est.cluster_centers_, est.labels_, est.inertia_, est.n_iter_\n   452→    else:\n   453→        return est.cluster_centers_, est.labels_, est.inertia_\n   454→\n   455→\n   456→def _kmeans_single_elkan(\n   457→    X,\n   458→    sample_weight,\n   459→    centers_init,\n   460→    max_iter=300,\n   461→    verbose=False,\n   462→    tol=1e-4,\n   463→    n_threads=1,\n   464→):\n   465→    \"\"\"A single run of k-means elkan, assumes preparation completed prior.\n   466→\n   467→    Parameters\n   468→    ----------\n   469→    X : {ndarray, sparse matrix} of shape (n_samples, n_features)\n   470→        The observations to cluster. If sparse matrix, must be in CSR format.\n   471→\n   472→    sample_weight : array-like of shape (n_samples,)\n   473→        The weights for each observation in X.\n   474→\n   475→    centers_init : ndarray of shape (n_clusters, n_features)\n   476→        The initial centers.\n   477→\n   478→    max_iter : int, default=300\n   479→        Maximum number of iterations of the k-means algorithm to run.\n   480→\n   481→    verbose : bool, default=False\n   482→        Verbosity mode.\n   483→\n   484→    tol : float, default=1e-4\n   485→        Relative tolerance with regards to Frobenius norm of the difference\n   486→        in the cluster centers of two consecutive iterations to declare\n   487→        convergence.\n   488→        It's not advised to set `tol=0` since convergence might never be\n   489→        declared due to rounding errors. Use a very small number instead.\n   490→\n   491→    n_threads : int, default=1\n   492→        The number of OpenMP threads to use for the computation. Parallelism is\n   493→        sample-wise on the main cython loop which assigns each sample to its\n   494→        closest center.\n   495→\n   496→    Returns\n   497→    -------\n   498→    centroid : ndarray of shape (n_clusters, n_features)\n   499→        Centroids found at the last iteration of k-means.\n   500→\n   501→    label : ndarray of shape (n_samples,)\n   502→        label[i] is the code or index of the centroid the\n   503→        i'th observation is closest to.\n   504→\n   505→    inertia : float\n   506→        The final value of the inertia criterion (sum of squared distances to\n   507→        the closest centroid for all observations in the training set).\n   508→\n   509→    n_iter : int\n   510→        Number of iterations run.\n   511→    \"\"\"\n   512→    n_samples = X.shape[0]\n   513→    n_clusters = centers_init.shape[0]\n   514→\n   515→    # Buffers to avoid new allocations at each iteration.\n   516→    centers = centers_init\n   517→    centers_new = np.zeros_like(centers)\n   518→    weight_in_clusters = np.zeros(n_clusters, dtype=X.dtype)\n   519→    labels = np.full(n_samples, -1, dtype=np.int32)\n   520→    labels_old = labels.copy()\n   521→    center_half_distances = euclidean_distances(centers) / 2\n   522→    distance_next_center = np.partition(\n   523→        np.asarray(center_half_distances), kth=1, axis=0\n   524→    )[1]\n   525→    upper_bounds = np.zeros(n_samples, dtype=X.dtype)\n   526→    lower_bounds = np.zeros((n_samples, n_clusters), dtype=X.dtype)\n   527→    center_shift = np.zeros(n_clusters, dtype=X.dtype)\n   528→\n   529→    if sp.issparse(X):\n   530→        init_bounds = init_bounds_sparse\n   531→        elkan_iter = elkan_iter_chunked_sparse\n   532→        _inertia = _inertia_sparse\n   533→    else:\n   534→        init_bounds = init_bounds_dense\n   535→        elkan_iter = elkan_iter_chunked_dense\n   536→        _inertia = _inertia_dense\n   537→\n   538→    init_bounds(\n   539→        X,\n   540→        centers,\n   541→        center_half_distances,\n   542→        labels,\n   543→        upper_bounds,\n   544→        lower_bounds,\n   545→        n_threads=n_threads,\n   546→    )\n   547→\n   548→    strict_convergence = False\n   549→\n   550→    for i in range(max_iter):\n   551→        elkan_iter(\n   552→            X,\n   553→            sample_weight,\n   554→            centers,\n   555→            centers_new,\n   556→            weight_in_clusters,\n   557→            center_half_distances,\n   558→            distance_next_center,\n   559→            upper_bounds,\n   560→            lower_bounds,\n   561→            labels,\n   562→            center_shift,\n   563→            n_threads,\n   564→        )\n   565→\n   566→        # compute new pairwise distances between centers and closest other\n   567→        # center of each center for next iterations\n   568→        center_half_distances = euclidean_distances(centers_new) / 2\n   569→        distance_next_center = np.partition(\n   570→            np.asarray(center_half_distances), kth=1, axis=0\n   571→        )[1]\n   572→\n   573→        if verbose:\n   574→            inertia = _inertia(X, sample_weight, centers, labels, n_threads)\n   575→            print(f\"Iteration {i}, inertia {inertia}\")\n   576→\n   577→        centers, centers_new = centers_new, centers\n   578→\n   579→        if np.array_equal(labels, labels_old):\n   580→            # First check the labels for strict convergence.\n   581→            if verbose:\n   582→                print(f\"Converged at iteration {i}: strict convergence.\")\n   583→            strict_convergence = True\n   584→            break\n   585→        else:\n   586→            # No strict convergence, check for tol based convergence.\n   587→            center_shift_tot = (center_shift**2).sum()\n   588→            if center_shift_tot <= tol:\n   589→                if verbose:\n   590→                    print(\n   591→                        f\"Converged at iteration {i}: center shift \"\n   592→                        f\"{center_shift_tot} within tolerance {tol}.\"\n   593→                    )\n   594→                break\n   595→\n   596→        labels_old[:] = labels\n   597→\n   598→    if not strict_convergence:\n   599→        # rerun E-step so that predicted labels match cluster centers\n   600→        elkan_iter(\n   601→            X,\n   602→            sample_weight,\n   603→            centers,\n   604→            centers,\n   605→            weight_in_clusters,\n   606→            center_half_distances,\n   607→            distance_next_center,\n   608→            upper_bounds,\n   609→            lower_bounds,\n   610→            labels,\n   611→            center_shift,\n   612→            n_threads,\n   613→            update_centers=False,\n   614→        )\n   615→\n   616→    inertia = _inertia(X, sample_weight, centers, labels, n_threads)\n   617→\n   618→    return labels, inertia, centers, i + 1\n   619→\n   620→\n   621→# Threadpoolctl context to limit the number of threads in second level of\n   622→# nested parallelism (i.e. BLAS) to avoid oversubscription.\n   623→@_threadpool_controller_decorator(limits=1, user_api=\"blas\")\n   624→def _kmeans_single_lloyd(\n   625→    X,\n   626→    sample_weight,\n   627→    centers_init,\n   628→    max_iter=300,\n   629→    verbose=False,\n   630→    tol=1e-4,\n   631→    n_threads=1,\n   632→):\n   633→    \"\"\"A single run of k-means lloyd, assumes preparation completed prior.\n   634→\n   635→    Parameters\n   636→    ----------\n   637→    X : {ndarray, sparse matrix} of shape (n_samples, n_features)\n   638→        The observations to cluster. Use a very small number instead.\n   658→\n   659→    n_threads : int, default=1\n   660→        The number of OpenMP threads to use for the computation. Parallelism is\n   661→        sample-wise on the main cython loop which assigns each sample to its\n   662→        closest center.\n   663→\n   664→    Returns\n   665→    -------\n   666→    centroid : ndarray of shape (n_clusters, n_features)\n   667→        Centroids found at the last iteration of k-means.\n   668→\n   669→    label : ndarray of shape (n_samples,)\n   670→        label[i] is the code or index of the centroid the\n   671→        i'th observation is closest to.\n   672→\n   673→    inertia : float\n   674→        The final value of the inertia criterion (sum of squared distances to\n   675→        the closest centroid for all observations in the training set).\n   676→\n   677→    n_iter : int\n   678→        Number of iterations run.\n   679→    \"\"\"\n   680→    n_clusters = centers_init.shape[0]\n   681→\n   682→    # Buffers to avoid new allocations at each iteration.\n   683→    centers = centers_init\n   684→    centers_new = np.zeros_like(centers)\n   685→    labels = np.full(X.shape[0], -1, dtype=np.int32)\n   686→    labels_old = labels.copy()\n   687→    weight_in_clusters = np.zeros(n_clusters, dtype=X.dtype)\n   688→    center_shift = np.zeros(n_clusters, dtype=X.dtype)\n   689→\n   690→    if sp.issparse(X):\n   691→        lloyd_iter = lloyd_iter_chunked_sparse\n   692→        _inertia = _inertia_sparse\n   693→    else:\n   694→        lloyd_iter = lloyd_iter_chunked_dense\n   695→        _inertia = _inertia_dense\n   696→\n   697→    strict_convergence = False\n   698→\n   699→    for i in range(max_iter):\n   700→        lloyd_iter(\n   701→            X,\n   702→            sample_weight,\n   703→            centers,\n   704→            centers_new,\n   705→            weight_in_clusters,\n   706→            labels,\n   707→            center_shift,\n   708→            n_threads,\n   709→        )\n   710→\n   711→        if verbose:\n   712→            inertia = _inertia(X, sample_weight, centers, labels, n_threads)\n   713→            print(f\"Iteration {i}, inertia {inertia}.\")\n   714→\n   715→        centers, centers_new = centers_new, centers\n   716→\n   717→        if np.array_equal(labels, labels_old):\n   718→            # First check the labels for strict convergence.\n   719→            if verbose:\n   720→                print(f\"Converged at iteration {i}: strict convergence.\")\n   721→            strict_convergence = True\n   722→            break\n   723→        else:\n   724→            # No strict convergence, check for tol based convergence.\n   725→            center_shift_tot = (center_shift**2).sum()\n   726→            if center_shift_tot <= tol:\n   727→                if verbose:\n   728→                    print(\n   729→                        f\"Converged at iteration {i}: center shift \"\n   730→                        f\"{center_shift_tot} within tolerance {tol}.\"\n   731→                    )\n   732→                break\n   733→\n   734→        labels_old[:] = labels\n   735→\n   736→    if not strict_convergence:\n   737→        # If sparse matrix, must\n   764→        be in CSR format.\n   765→\n   766→    sample_weight : ndarray of shape (n_samples,)\n   767→        The weights for each observation in X.\n   768→\n   769→    x_squared_norms : ndarray of shape (n_samples,)\n   770→        Precomputed squared euclidean norm of each data point, to speed up\n   771→        computations.\n   772→\n   773→    centers : ndarray of shape (n_clusters, n_features)\n   774→        The cluster centers.\n   775→\n   776→    n_threads : int, default=1\n   777→        The number of OpenMP threads to use for the computation. Parallelism is\n   778→        sample-wise on the main cython loop which assigns each sample to its\n   779→        closest center.\n   780→\n   781→    return_inertia : bool, default=True\n   782→        Whether to compute and return the inertia.\n   783→\n   784→    Returns\n   785→    -------\n   786→    labels : ndarray of shape (n_samples,)\n   787→        The resulting assignment.\n   788→\n   789→    inertia : float\n   790→        Sum of squared distances of samples to their closest cluster center.\n   791→        Inertia is only returned if return_inertia is True.\n   792→    \"\"\"\n   793→    n_samples = X.shape[0]\n   794→    n_clusters = centers.shape[0]\n   795→\n   796→    labels = np.full(n_samples, -1, dtype=np.int32)\n   797→    center_shift = np.zeros(n_clusters, dtype=centers.dtype)\n   798→\n   799→    if sp.issparse(X):\n   800→        _labels = lloyd_iter_chunked_sparse\n   801→        _inertia = _inertia_sparse\n   802→    else:\n   803→        _labels = This technique speeds up \\\n  1208→            convergence. The algorithm implemented is \"greedy k-means++\". It \\\n  1209→            differs from the vanilla k-means++ by making several trials at \\\n  1210→            each sampling step and choosing the best centroid among them.\n  1211→\n  1212→        * 'random': choose `n_clusters` observations (rows) at random from \\\n  1213→        data for the initial centroids.\n  1214→\n  1215→        * If an array is passed, it should be of shape (n_clusters, n_features)\\\n  1216→        and gives the initial centers.\n  1217→\n  1218→        * If a callable is passed, it should take arguments X, n_clusters and a\\\n  1219→        random state and return an initialization.\n  1220→\n  1221→        For an example of how to use the different `init` strategies, see\n  1222→        :ref:`sphx_glr_auto_examples_cluster_plot_kmeans_digits.py`.\n  1223→\n  1224→        For an evaluation of the impact of initialization, see the example\n  1225→        :ref:`sphx_glr_auto_examples_cluster_plot_kmeans_stability_low_dim_dense.py`.\n  1226→\n  1227→    n_init : 'auto' or int, default='auto'\n  1228→        Number of times the k-means algorithm is run with different centroid\n  1229→        seeds. The final results is the best output of `n_init` consecutive runs\n  1230→        in terms of inertia. Several runs are recommended for sparse\n  1231→        high-dimensional problems (see :ref:`kmeans_sparse_high_dim`).\n  1232→\n  1233→        When `n_init='auto'`, the number of runs depends on the value of init:\n  1234→        10 if using `init='random'` or `init` is a callable;\n  1235→        1 if using `init='k-means++'` or `init` is an array-like.\n  1236→\n  1237→        .. versionadded:: 1.2\n  1238→           Added 'auto' option for `n_init`.\n  1239→\n  1240→        .. versionchanged:: 1.4\n  1241→           Default value for `n_init` changed to `'auto'`.\n  1242→\n  1243→    max_iter : int, default=300\n  1244→        Maximum number of iterations of the k-means algorithm for a\n  1245→        single run.\n  1246→\n  1247→    tol : float, default=1e-4\n  1248→        Relative tolerance with regards to Frobenius norm of the difference\n  1249→        in the cluster centers of two consecutive iterations to declare\n  1250→        convergence.\n  1251→\n  1252→    verbose : int, default=0\n  1253→        Verbosity mode.\n  1254→\n  1255→    random_state : int, RandomState instance or None, default=None\n  1256→        Determines random number generation for centroid initialization. Use\n  1257→        an int to make the randomness deterministic.\n  1258→        See :term:`Glossary <random_state>`.\n  1259→\n  1260→    copy_x : bool, default=True\n  1261→        When pre-computing distances it is more numerically accurate to center\n  1262→        the data first. If copy_x is True (default), then the original data is\n  1263→        not modified. If False, the original data is modified, and put back\n  1264→        before the function returns, but small numerical differences may be\n  1265→        introduced by subtracting and then adding the data mean. Note that if\n  1266→        the original data is not C-contiguous, a copy will be made even if\n  1267→        copy_x is False. If the original data is sparse, but not in CSR format,\n  1268→        a copy will be made even if copy_x is False.\n  1269→\n  1270→    algorithm : {\"lloyd\", \"elkan\"}, default=\"lloyd\"\n  1271→        K-means algorithm to use. The classical EM-style algorithm is `\"lloyd\"`.\n  1272→        The `\"elkan\"` variation can be more efficient on some datasets with\n  1273→        well-defined clusters, by using the triangle inequality. However it's\n  1274→        more memory intensive due to the allocation of an extra array of shape\n  1275→        `(n_samples, n_clusters)`.\n  1276→\n  1277→        .. versionchanged:: 0.18\n  1278→            Added Elkan algorithm\n  1279→\n  1280→        .. versionchanged:: 1.1\n  1281→            Renamed \"full\" to \"lloyd\", and deprecated \"auto\" and \"full\".\n  1282→            Changed \"auto\" to use \"lloyd\" instead of \"elkan\".\n  1283→\n  1284→    Attributes\n  1285→    ----------\n  1286→    cluster_centers_ : ndarray of shape (n_clusters, n_features)\n  1287→        Coordinates of cluster centers. If the algorithm stops before fully\n  1288→        converging (see ``tol`` and ``max_iter``), these will not be\n  1289→        consistent with ``labels_``.\n  1290→\n  1291→    labels_ : ndarray of shape (n_samples,)\n  1292→        Labels of each point\n  1293→\n  1294→    inertia_ : float\n  1295→        Sum of squared distances of samples to their closest cluster center,\n  1296→        weighted by the sample weights if provided.\n  1297→\n  1298→    n_iter_ : int\n  1299→        Number of iterations run.\n  1300→\n  1301→    n_features_in_ : int\n  1302→        Number of features seen during :term:`fit`.\n  1303→\n  1304→        .. versionadded:: 0.24\n  1305→\n  1306→    feature_names_in_ : ndarray of shape (`n_features_in_`,)\n  1307→        Names of features seen during :term:`fit`. Defined only when `X`\n  1308→        has feature names that are all strings.\n  1309→\n  1310→        .. versionadded:: 1.0\n  1311→\n  1312→    See Also\n  1313→    --------\n  1314→    MiniBatchKMeans : Alternative online implementation that does incremental\n  1315→        updates of the centers positions using mini-batches.\n  1316→        For large scale learning (say n_samples > 10k) MiniBatchKMeans is\n  1317→        probably much faster than the default batch implementation.\n  1318→\n  1319→    Notes\n  1320→    -----\n  1321→    The k-means problem is solved using either Lloyd's or Elkan's algorithm.\n  1322→\n  1323→    The average complexity is given by O(k n T), where n is the number of\n  1324→    samples and T is the number of iteration.\n  1325→\n  1326→    The worst case complexity is given by O(n^(k+2/p)) with\n  1327→    n = n_samples, p = n_features.\n  1328→    Refer to :doi:`\"How slow is the k-means method?\" D. Arthur and S. Vassilvitskii -\n  1329→    SoCG2006.<10.1145/1137856.1137880>` for more details.\n  1330→\n  1331→    In practice, the k-means algorithm is very fast (one of the fastest\n  1332→    clustering algorithms available), but it falls in local minima. That's why\n  1333→    it can be useful to restart it several times.\n  1334→\n  1335→    If the algorithm stops before fully converging (because of ``tol`` or\n  1336→    ``max_iter``), ``labels_`` and ``cluster_centers_`` will not be consistent,\n  1337→    i.e. the ``cluster_centers_`` will not be the means of the points in each\n  1338→    cluster. Also, the estimator will reassign ``labels_`` after the last\n  1339→    iteration to make ``labels_`` consistent with ``predict`` on the training\n  1340→    set.\n  1341→\n  1342→    Examples\n  1343→    --------\n  1344→\n  1345→    >>> from sklearn.cluster import KMeans\n  1346→    >>> import numpy as np\n  1347→    >>> X = np.array([[1, 2], [1, 4], [1, 0],\n  1348→    ...               [10, 2], [10, 4], [10, 0]])\n  1349→    >>> kmeans = KMeans(n_clusters=2, random_state=0, n_init=\"auto\").fit(X)\n  1350→    >>> kmeans.labels_\n  1351→    array([1, 1, 1, 0, 0, 0], dtype=int32)\n  1352→    >>> kmeans.predict([[0, 0], [12, 3]])\n  1353→    array([1, 0], dtype=int32)\n  1354→    >>> kmeans.cluster_centers_\n  1355→    array([[10.,  2.],\n  1356→           [ 1.,  2.]])\n  1357→\n  1358→    For examples of common problems with K-Means and how to address them see\n  1359→    :ref:`sphx_glr_auto_examples_cluster_plot_kmeans_assumptions.py`.\n  1360→\n  1361→    For a demonstration of how K-Means can be used to cluster text documents see\n  1362→    :ref:`sphx_glr_auto_examples_text_plot_document_clustering.py`.\n  1363→\n  1364→    For a comparison between K-Means and MiniBatchKMeans refer to example\n  1365→    :ref:`sphx_glr_auto_examples_cluster_plot_mini_batch_kmeans.py`.\n  1366→\n  1367→    For a comparison between K-Means and BisectingKMeans refer to example\n  1368→    :ref:`sphx_glr_auto_examples_cluster_plot_bisect_kmeans.py`.\n  1369→    \"\"\"\n  1370→\n  1371→    _parameter_constraints: dict = {\n  1372→        **_BaseKMeans._parameter_constraints,\n  1373→        \"copy_x\": [\"boolean\"],\n  1374→        \"algorithm\": [StrOptions({\"lloyd\", \"elkan\"})],\n  1375→    }\n  1376→\n  1377→    def __init__(\n  1378→        self,\n  1379→        n_clusters=8,\n  1380→        *,\n  1381→        init=\"k-means++\",\n  1382→        n_init=\"auto\",\n  1383→        max_iter=300,\n  1384→        tol=1e-4,\n  1385→        verbose=0,\n  1386→        random_state=None,\n  1387→        copy_x=True,\n  1388→        algorithm=\"lloyd\",\n  1389→    ):\n  1390→        super().__init__(\n  1391→            n_clusters=n_clusters,\n  1392→            init=init,\n  1393→            n_init=n_init,\n  1394→            max_iter=max_iter,\n  1395→            tol=tol,\n  1396→            verbose=verbose,\n  1397→            random_state=random_state,\n  1398→        )\n  1399→\n  1400→        self.copy_x = copy_x\n  1401→        self.algorithm = algorithm\n  1402→\n  1403→    def _check_params_vs_input(self, X):\n  1404→        super()._check_params_vs_input(X, default_n_init=10)\n  1405→\n  1406→        self._algorithm = self.algorithm\n  1407→        if self._algorithm == \"elkan\" and self.n_clusters == 1:\n  1408→            warnings.warn(\n  1409→                (\n  1410→                    \"algorithm='elkan' doesn't make sense for a single \"\n  1411→                    \"cluster. Using 'lloyd' instead.\"\n  1412→                ),\n  1413→                RuntimeWarning,\n  1414→            )\n  1415→            self._algorithm = \"lloyd\"\n  1416→\n  1417→    def _warn_mkl_vcomp(self, n_active_threads):\n  1418→        \"\"\"Warn when vcomp and mkl are both present\"\"\"\n  1419→        warnings.warn(\n  1420→            \"KMeans is known to have a memory leak on Windows \"\n  1421→            \"with MKL, when there are less chunks than available \"\n  1422→            \"threads. You can avoid it by setting the environment\"\n  1423→            f\" variable OMP_NUM_THREADS={n_active_threads}.\"\n  1424→        )\n  1425→\n  1426→    @_fit_context(prefer_skip_nested_validation=True)\n  1427→    def fit(self, X, y=None, sample_weight=None):\n  1428→        \"\"\"Compute k-means clustering.\n  1429→\n  1430→        Parameters\n  1431→        ----------\n  1432→        X : {array-like, sparse matrix} of shape (n_samples, n_features)\n  1433→            Training instances to cluster. It must be noted that the data\n  1434→            will be converted to C ordering, which will cause a memory\n  1435→            copy if the given data is not C-contiguous.\n  1436→            If a sparse matrix is passed, a copy will be made if it's not in\n  1437→            CSR format.\n  1438→\n  1439→        y : Ignored\n  1440→            Not used, present here for API consistency by convention.\n  1441→\n  1442→        sample_weight : array-like of shape (n_samples,), default=None\n  1443→            The weights for each observation in X. If None, all observations\n  1444→            are assigned equal weight. `sample_weight` is not used during\n  1445→            initialization if `init` is a callable or a user provided array.\n  1446→\n  1447→            .. versionadded:: 0.20\n  1448→\n  1449→        Returns\n  1450→        -------\n  1451→        self : object\n  1452→            Fitted estimator.\n  1453→        \"\"\"\n  1454→        X = validate_data(\n  1455→            self,\n  1456→            X,\n  1457→            accept_sparse=\"csr\",\n  1458→            dtype=[np.float64, np.float32],\n  1459→            order=\"C\",\n  1460→            copy=self.copy_x,\n  1461→            accept_large_sparse=False,\n  1462→        )\n  1463→\n  1464→        self._check_params_vs_input(X)\n  1465→\n  1466→        random_state = check_random_state(self.random_state)\n  1467→        sample_weight = _check_sample_weight(sample_weight, X, dtype=X.dtype)\n  1468→        self._n_threads = _openmp_effective_n_threads()\n  1469→\n  1470→        # Validate init array\n  1471→        init = self.init\n  1472→        init_is_array_like = _is_arraylike_not_scalar(init)\n  1473→        if init_is_array_like:\n  1474→            init = check_array(init, dtype=X.dtype, copy=True, order=\"C\")\n  1475→            self._validate_center_shape(X, init)\n  1476→\n  1477→        # subtract of mean of x for more accurate distance computations\n  1478→        if not sp.issparse(X):\n  1479→            X_mean = X.mean(axis=0)\n  1480→            # The copy was already done above\n  1481→            X -= X_mean\n  1482→\n  1483→            if init_is_array_like:\n  1484→                init -= X_mean\n  1485→\n  1486→        # precompute squared norms of data points\n  1487→        x_squared_norms = row_norms(X, squared=True)\n  1488→\n  1489→        if self._algorithm == \"elkan\":\n  1490→            kmeans_single = _kmeans_single_elkan\n  1491→        else:\n  1492→            kmeans_single = _kmeans_single_lloyd\n  1493→            self._check_mkl_vcomp(X, X.shape[0])\n  1494→\n  1495→        best_inertia, best_labels = None, None\n  1496→\n  1497→        for i in range(self._n_init):\n  1498→            # Initialize centers\n  1499→            centers_init = self._init_centroids(\n  1500→                X,\n  1501→                x_squared_norms=x_squared_norms,\n  1502→                init=init,\n  1503→                random_state=random_state,\n  1504→                sample_weight=sample_weight,\n  1505→            )\n  1506→            if self.verbose:\n  1507→                print(\"Initialization complete\")\n  1508→\n  1509→            # run a k-means once\n  1510→            labels, inertia, centers, n_iter_ = kmeans_single(\n  1511→                X,\n  1512→                sample_weight,\n  1513→                centers_init,\n  1514→                max_iter=self.max_iter,\n  1515→                verbose=self.verbose,\n  1516→                tol=self._tol,\n  1517→                n_threads=self._n_threads,\n  1518→            )\n  1519→\n  1520→            # determine if these results are the best so far\n  1521→            # we chose a new run if it has a better inertia and the clustering is\n  1522→            # different from the best so far (it's possible that the inertia is\n  1523→            # slightly better even if the clustering is the same with potentially\n  1524→            # permuted labels, due to rounding errors)\n  1525→            if best_inertia is None or (\n  1526→                inertia < best_inertia\n  1527→                and not _is_same_clustering(labels, best_labels, self.n_clusters)\n  1528→            ):\n  1529→                best_labels = labels\n  1530→                best_centers = centers\n  1531→                best_inertia = inertia\n  1532→                best_n_iter = n_iter_\n  1533→\n  1534→        if not sp.issparse(X):\n  1535→            if not self.copy_x:\n  1536→                X += X_mean\n  1537→            best_centers += X_mean\n  1538→\n  1539→        distinct_clusters = len(set(best_labels))\n  1540→        if distinct_clusters < self.n_clusters:\n  1541→            warnings.warn(\n  1542→                \"Number of distinct clusters ({}) found smaller than \"\n  1543→                \"n_clusters ({}). Possibly due to duplicate points \"\n  1544→                \"in X.\".format(distinct_clusters, self.n_clusters),\n  1545→                ConvergenceWarning,\n  1546→                stacklevel=2,\n  1547→            )\n  1548→\n  1549→        self.cluster_centers_ = best_centers\n  1550→        self._n_features_out = self.cluster_centers_.shape[0]\n  1551→        self.labels_ = best_labels\n  1552→        self.inertia_ = best_inertia\n  1553→        self.n_iter_ = best_n_iter\n  1554→        return self\n  1555→\n  1556→\n  1557→def _mini_batch_step(\n  1558→    X,\n  1559→    sample_weight,\n  1560→    centers,\n  1561→    centers_new,\n  1562→    weight_sums,\n  1563→    random_state,\n  1564→    random_reassign=False,\n  1565→    reassignment_ratio=0.01,\n  1566→    verbose=False,\n  1567→    n_threads=1,\n  1568→):\n  1569→    \"\"\"Incremental update of the centers for the Minibatch K-Means algorithm.\n  1570→\n  1571→    Parameters\n  1572→    ----------\n  1573→\n  1574→    X : {ndarray, sparse matrix} of shape (n_samples, n_features)\n  1575→        The original data array. If sparse, must be in CSR format.\n  1576→\n  1577→    x_squared_norms : ndarray of shape (n_samples,)\n  1578→        Squared euclidean norm of each data point.\n  1579→\n  1580→    sample_weight : ndarray of shape (n_samples,)\n  1581→        The weights for each observation in `X`.\n  1582→\n  1583→    centers : ndarray of shape (n_clusters, n_features)\n  1584→        The cluster centers before the current iteration\n  1585→\n  1586→    centers_new : ndarray of shape (n_clusters, n_features)\n  1587→        The cluster centers after the current iteration. Modified in-place.\n  1588→\n  1589→    weight_sums : ndarray of shape (n_clusters,)\n  1590→        The vector in which we keep track of the numbers of points in a\n  1591→        cluster. This array is modified in place.\n  1592→\n  1593→    random_state : RandomState instance\n  1594→        Determines random number generation for low count centers reassignment.\n  1595→        See :term:`Glossary <random_state>`.\n  1596→\n  1597→    random_reassign : boolean, default=False\n  1598→        If True, centers with very low counts are randomly reassigned\n  1599→        to observations.\n  1600→\n  1601→    reassignment_ratio : float, default=0.01\n  1602→        Control the fraction of the maximum number of counts for a\n  1603→        center to be reassigned. A higher value means that low count\n  1604→        centers are more likely to be reassigned, which means that the\n  1605→        model will take longer to converge, but should converge in a\n  1606→        better clustering.\n  1607→\n  1608→    verbose : bool, default=False\n  1609→        Controls the verbosity.\n  1610→\n  1611→    n_threads : int, default=1\n  1612→        The number of OpenMP threads to use for the computation.\n  1613→\n  1614→    Returns\n  1615→    -------\n  1616→    inertia : float\n  1617→        Sum of squared distances of samples to their closest cluster center.\n  1618→        The inertia is computed after finding the labels and before updating\n  1619→        the centers.\n  1620→    \"\"\"\n  1621→    # Perform label assignment to nearest centers\n  1622→    # For better efficiency, it's better to run _mini_batch_step in a\n  1623→    # threadpool_limit context than using _labels_inertia_threadpool_limit here\n  1624→    labels, inertia = _labels_inertia(X, sample_weight, centers, n_threads=n_threads)\n  1625→\n  1626→    # Update centers according to the labels\n  1627→    if sp.issparse(X):\n  1628→        _minibatch_update_sparse(\n  1629→            X, sample_weight, centers, centers_new, weight_sums, labels, n_threads\n  1630→        )\n  1631→    else:\n  1632→        _minibatch_update_dense(\n  1633→            X,\n  1634→            sample_weight,\n  1635→            centers,\n  1636→            centers_new,\n  1637→            weight_sums,\n  1638→            labels,\n  1639→            n_threads,\n  1640→        )\n  1641→\n  1642→    # Reassign clusters that have very low weight\n  1643→    if random_reassign and reassignment_ratio > 0:\n  1644→        to_reassign = weight_sums < reassignment_ratio * weight_sums.max()\n  1645→\n  1646→        # pick at most .5 * batch_size samples as new centers\n  1647→        if to_reassign.sum() > 0.5 * X.shape[0]:\n  1648→            indices_dont_reassign = np.argsort(weight_sums)[int(0.5 * X.shape[0]) :]\n  1649→            to_reassign[indices_dont_reassign] = False\n  1650→        n_reassigns = to_reassign.sum()\n  1651→\n  1652→        if n_reassigns:\n  1653→            # Pick new clusters amongst observations with uniform probability\n  1654→            new_centers = random_state.choice(\n  1655→                X.shape[0], replace=False, size=n_reassigns\n  1656→            )\n  1657→            if verbose:\n  1658→                print(f\"[MiniBatchKMeans] Reassigning {n_reassigns} cluster centers.\")\n  1659→\n  1660→            if sp.issparse(X):\n  1661→                assign_rows_csr(\n  1662→                    X,\n  1663→                    new_centers.astype(np.intp, copy=False),\n  1664→                    np.where(to_reassign)[0].astype(np.intp, copy=False),\n  1665→                    centers_new,\n  1666→                )\n  1667→            else:\n  1668→                centers_new[to_reassign] = X[new_centers]\n  1669→\n  1670→        # reset counts of reassigned centers, but don't reset them too small\n  1671→        # to avoid instant reassignment. This is a pretty dirty hack as it\n  1672→        # also modifies the learning rates.\n  1673→        weight_sums[to_reassign] = np.min(weight_sums[~to_reassign])\n  1674→\n  1675→    return inertia\n  1676→\n  1677→\n  1678→class MiniBatchKMeans(_BaseKMeans):\n  1679→    \"\"\"\n  1680→    Mini-Batch K-Means clustering.\n  1681→\n  1682→    Read more in the :ref:`User Guide <mini_batch_kmeans>`.\n  1683→\n  1684→    Parameters\n  1685→    ----------\n  1686→\n  1687→    n_clusters : int, default=8\n  1688→        The number of clusters to form as well as the number of\n  1689→        centroids to generate.\n  1690→\n  1691→    init : {'k-means++', 'random'}, callable or array-like of shape \\\n  1692→            (n_clusters, n_features), default='k-means++'\n  1693→        Method for initialization:\n  1694→\n  1695→        'k-means++' : selects initial cluster centroids using sampling based on\n  1696→        an empirical probability distribution of the points' contribution to the\n  1697→        overall inertia. This technique speeds up convergence. The algorithm\n  1698→        implemented is \"greedy k-means++\". It differs from the vanilla k-means++\n  1699→        by making several trials at each sampling step and choosing the best centroid\n  1700→        among them.\n  1701→\n  1702→        'random': choose `n_clusters` observations (rows) at random from data\n  1703→        for the initial centroids.\n  1704→\n  1705→        If an array is passed, it should be of shape (n_clusters, n_features)\n  1706→        and gives the initial centers.\n  1707→\n  1708→        If a callable is passed, it should take arguments X, n_clusters and a\n  1709→        random state and return an initialization.\n  1710→\n  1711→        For an evaluation of the impact of initialization, see the example\n  1712→        :ref:`sphx_glr_auto_examples_cluster_plot_kmeans_stability_low_dim_dense.py`.\n  1713→\n  1714→    max_iter : int, default=100\n  1715→        Maximum number of iterations over the complete dataset before\n  1716→        stopping independently of any early stopping criterion heuristics.\n  1717→\n  1718→    batch_size : int, default=1024\n  1719→        Size of the mini batches.\n  1720→        For faster computations, you can set the ``batch_size`` greater than\n  1721→        256 * number of cores to enable parallelism on all cores.\n  1722→\n  1723→        .. versionchanged:: 1.0\n  1724→           `batch_size` default changed from 100 to 1024.\n  1725→\n  1726→    verbose : int, default=0\n  1727→        Verbosity mode.\n  1728→\n  1729→    compute_labels : bool, default=True\n  1730→        Compute label assignment and inertia for the complete dataset\n  1731→        once the minibatch optimization has converged in fit.\n  1732→\n  1733→    random_state : int, RandomState instance or None, default=None\n  1734→        Determines random number generation for centroid initialization and\n  1735→        random reassignment. Use an int to make the randomness deterministic.\n  1736→        See :term:`Glossary <random_state>`.\n  1737→\n  1738→    tol : float, default=0.0\n  1739→        Control early stopping based on the relative center changes as\n  1740→        measured by a smoothed, variance-normalized of the mean center\n  1741→        squared position changes. This early stopping heuristics is\n  1742→        closer to the one used for the batch variant of the algorithms\n  1743→        but induces a slight computational and memory overhead over the\n  1744→        inertia heuristic.\n  1745→\n  1746→        To disable convergence detection based on normalized center\n  1747→        change, set tol to 0.0 (default).\n  1748→\n  1749→    max_no_improvement : int, default=10\n  1750→        Control early stopping based on the consecutive number of mini\n  1751→        batches that does not yield an improvement on the smoothed inertia.\n  1752→\n  1753→        To disable convergence detection based on inertia, set\n  1754→        max_no_improvement to None.\n  1755→\n  1756→    init_size : int, default=None\n  1757→        Number of samples to randomly sample for speeding up the\n  1758→        initialization (sometimes at the expense of accuracy): the\n  1759→        only algorithm is initialized by running a batch KMeans on a\n  1760→        random subset of the data. This needs to be larger than n_clusters.\n  1761→\n  1762→        If `None`, the heuristic is `init_size = 3 * batch_size` if\n  1763→        `3 * batch_size < n_clusters`, else `init_size = 3 * n_clusters`.\n  1764→\n  1765→    n_init : 'auto' or int, default=\"auto\"\n  1766→        Number of random initializations that are tried.\n  1767→        In contrast to KMeans, the algorithm is only run once, using the best of\n  1768→        the `n_init` initializations as measured by inertia. Several runs are\n  1769→        recommended for sparse high-dimensional problems (see\n  1770→        :ref:`kmeans_sparse_high_dim`).\n  1771→\n  1772→        When `n_init='auto'`, the number of runs depends on the value of init:\n  1773→        3 if using `init='random'` or `init` is a callable;\n  1774→        1 if using `init='k-means++'` or `init` is an array-like.\n  1775→\n  1776→        .. versionadded:: 1.2\n  1777→           Added 'auto' option for `n_init`.\n  1778→\n  1779→        .. versionchanged:: 1.4\n  1780→           Default value for `n_init` changed to `'auto'` in version.\n  1781→\n  1782→    reassignment_ratio : float, default=0.01\n  1783→        Control the fraction of the maximum number of counts for a center to\n  1784→        be reassigned. A higher value means that low count centers are more\n  1785→        easily reassigned, which means that the model will take longer to\n  1786→        converge, but should converge in a better clustering. However, too high\n  1787→        a value may cause convergence issues, especially with a small batch\n  1788→        size.\n  1789→\n  1790→    Attributes\n  1791→    ----------\n  1792→\n  1793→    cluster_centers_ : ndarray of shape (n_clusters, n_features)\n  1794→        Coordinates of cluster centers.\n  1795→\n  1796→    labels_ : ndarray of shape (n_samples,)\n  1797→        Labels of each point (if compute_labels is set to True).\n  1798→\n  1799→    inertia_ : float\n  1800→        The value of the inertia criterion associated with the chosen\n  1801→        partition if compute_labels is set to True. If compute_labels is set to\n  1802→        False, it's an approximation of the inertia based on an exponentially\n  1803→        weighted average of the batch inertiae.\n  1804→        The inertia is defined as the sum of square distances of samples to\n  1805→        their cluster center, weighted by the sample weights if provided.\n  1806→\n  1807→    n_iter_ : int\n  1808→        Number of iterations over the full dataset.\n  1809→\n  1810→    n_steps_ : int\n  1811→        Number of minibatches processed.\n  1812→\n  1813→        .. versionadded:: 1.0\n  1814→\n  1815→    n_features_in_ : int\n  1816→        Number of features seen during :term:`fit`.\n  1817→\n  1818→        .. versionadded:: 0.24\n  1819→\n  1820→    feature_names_in_ : ndarray of shape (`n_features_in_`,)\n  1821→        Names of features seen during :term:`fit`. Defined only when `X`\n  1822→        has feature names that are all strings.\n  1823→\n  1824→        .. versionadded:: 1.0\n  1825→\n  1826→    See Also\n  1827→    --------\n  1828→    KMeans : The classic implementation of the clustering method based on the\n  1829→        Lloyd's algorithm. It consumes the whole set of input data at each\n  1830→        iteration.\n  1831→\n  1832→    Notes\n  1833→    -----\n  1834→    See https://www.eecs.tufts.edu/~dsculley/papers/fastkmeans.pdf\n  1835→\n  1836→    When there are too few points in the dataset, some centers may be\n  1837→    duplicated, which means that a proper clustering in terms of the number\n  1838→    of requesting clusters and the number of returned clusters will not\n  1839→    always match. One solution is to set `reassignment_ratio=0`, which\n  1840→    prevents reassignments of clusters that are too small.\n  1841→\n  1842→    See :ref:`sphx_glr_auto_examples_cluster_plot_birch_vs_minibatchkmeans.py` for a\n  1843→    comparison with :class:`~sklearn.cluster.BIRCH`.\n  1844→\n  1845→    Examples\n  1846→    --------\n  1847→    >>> from sklearn.cluster import MiniBatchKMeans\n  1848→    >>> import numpy as np\n  1849→    >>> X = np.array([[1, 2], [1, 4], [1, 0],\n  1850→    ...               [4, 2], [4, 0], [4, 4],\n  1851→    ...               [4, 5], [0, 1], [2, 2],\n  1852→    ...               [3, 2], [5, 5], [1, -1]])\n  1853→    >>> # manually fit on batches\n  1854→    >>> kmeans = MiniBatchKMeans(n_clusters=2,\n  1855→    ...                          random_state=0,\n  1856→    ...                          batch_size=6,\n  1857→    ...                          n_init=\"auto\")\n  1858→    >>> kmeans = kmeans.partial_fit(X[0:6,:])\n  1859→    >>> kmeans = kmeans.partial_fit(X[6:12,:])\n  1860→    >>> kmeans.cluster_centers_\n  1861→    array([[3.375, 3.  ],\n  1862→           [0.75 , 0.5 ]])\n  1863→    >>> kmeans.predict([[0, 0], [4, 4]])\n  1864→    array([1, 0], dtype=int32)\n  1865→    >>> # fit on the whole data\n  1866→    >>> kmeans = MiniBatchKMeans(n_clusters=2,\n  1867→    ...                          random_state=0,\n  1868→    ...                          batch_size=6,\n  1869→    ...                          max_iter=10,\n  1870→    ...                          n_init=\"auto\").fit(X)\n  1871→    >>> kmeans.cluster_centers_\n  1872→    array([[3.55102041, 2.48979592],\n  1873→           [1.06896552, 1.        ]])\n  1874→    >>> kmeans.predict([[0, 0], [4, 4]])\n  1875→    array([1, 0], dtype=int32)\n  1876→\n  1877→    For a comparison of Mini-Batch K-Means clustering with other clustering algorithms,\n  1878→    see :ref:`sphx_glr_auto_examples_cluster_plot_cluster_comparison.py`\n  1879→    \"\"\"\n  1880→\n  1881→ For a\n        given index and center, X[index] = center.\n\n    Notes\n    -----\n    Selects initial cluster centers for k-mean clustering in a smart way\n    to speed up convergence. see: Arthur, D. and Vassilvitskii, S.\n    \"k-means++: the advantages of careful seeding\". ACM-SIAM symposium\n    on Discrete algorithms. 2007\n\n    Examples\n    --------\n\n    >>> from sklearn.cluster import kmeans_plusplus\n    >>> import numpy as np\n    >>> X = np.array([[1, 2], [1, 4], [1, 0],\n    ...               [10, 2], [10, 4], [10, 0]])\n    >>> centers, indices = kmeans_plusplus(X, n_clusters=2, random_state=0)\n    >>> centers\n    array([[10,  2],\n           [ 1,  0]])\n    >>> indices\n    array([3, 2])\n    \"\"\"\n    # Check data\n    check_array(X, accept_sparse=\"csr\", dtype=[np.float64, np.float32])\n    sample_weight = _check_sample_weight(sample_weight, X, dtype=X.dtype)\n\n    if X.shape[0] < n_clusters:\n        raise ValueError(\n            f\"n_samples={X.shape[0]} should be >= n_clusters={n_clusters}.\"\n        )\n\n    # Check parameters\n    if x_squared_norms is None:\n        x_squared_norms = row_norms(X, squared=True)\n    else:\n        x_squared_norms = check_array(x_squared_norms, dtype=X.dtype, ensure_2d=False)\n\n    if x_squared_norms.shape[0] != X.shape[0]:\n        raise ValueError(\n            f\"The length of x_squared_norms {x_squared_norms.shape[0]} should \"\n            f\"be equal to the length of n_samples {X.shape[0]}.\"\n        )\n\n    random_state = check_random_state(random_state)\n\n    # Call private k-means++\n    centers, indices = _kmeans_plusplus(\n        X, n_clusters, x_squared_norms, sample_weight, random_state, n_local_trials\n    )\n\n    return centers, indices\n\n\ndef _kmeans_plusplus(\n    X, n_clusters, x_squared_norms, sample_weight, random_state, n_local_trials=None\n):\n    \"\"\"Computational component for initialization of n_clusters by\n    k-means++. Prior validation of data is assumed.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    X : {ndarray, sparse matrix} of shape (n_samples, n_features)\n        The data to pick seeds for.\n\n    n_clusters : int\n        The number of seeds to choose.\n\n    sample_weight : ndarray of shape (n_samples,)\n        The weights for each observation in `X`.\n\n    x_squared_norms : ndarray of shape (n_samples,)\n        Squared Euclidean norm of each data point.\n\n    random_state : RandomState instance\n        The generator used to initialize the centers.\n        See :term:`Glossary <random_state>`.\n\n    n_local_trials : int, default=None\n        The number of seeding trials for each center (except the first),\n        of which the one reducing inertia the most is greedily chosen.\n        Set to None to make the number of trials depend logarithmically\n        on the number of seeds (2+log(k)); this is the default.\n\n    Returns\n    -------\n    centers : ndarray of shape (n_clusters, n_features)\n        The initial centers for k-means.\n\n    indices : ndarray of shape (n_clusters,)\n        The index location of the chosen centers in the data array X. For a\n        given index and center, X[index] = center.\n    \"\"\"\n    n_samples, n_features = X.shape\n\n    centers = np.empty((n_clusters, n_features), dtype=X.dtype)\n\n    # Set the number of local seeding trials if none is given\n    if n_local_trials is None:\n        # This is what Arthur/Vassilvitskii tried, but did not report\n        # specific results for other than mentioning in the conclusion\n        # that it helped.\n        n_local_trials = 2 + int(np.log(n_clusters))\n\n    # Pick first center randomly and track index of point\n    center_id = random_state.choice(n_samples, p=sample_weight / sample_weight.sum())\n    indices = np.full(n_clusters, -1, dtype=int)\n    if sp.issparse(X):\n        centers[0] = X[[center_id]].toarray()\n    else:\n        centers[0] = X[center_id]\n    indices[0] = center_id\n\n    # Initialize list of closest distances and calculate current potential\n    closest_dist_sq = _euclidean_distances(\n        centers[0, np.newaxis], X, Y_norm_squared=x_squared_norms, squared=True\n    )\n    current_pot = closest_dist_sq @ sample_weight\n\n    # Pick the remaining n_clusters-1 points\n    for c in range(1, n_clusters):\n        # Choose center candidates by sampling with probability proportional\n        # to the squared distance to the closest existing center\n        rand_vals = random_state.uniform(size=n_local_trials) * current_pot\n        candidate_ids = np.searchsorted(\n            stable_cumsum(sample_weight * closest_dist_sq), rand_vals\n        )\n        # XXX: numerical imprecision can result in a candidate_id out of range\n        np.clip(candidate_ids, None, closest_dist_sq.size - 1, out=candidate_ids)\n\n        # Compute distances to center candidates\n        distance_to_candidates = _euclidean_distances(\n            X[candidate_ids], X, Y_norm_squared=x_squared_norms, squared=True\n        )\n\n        # update closest distances squared and potential for each candidate\n        np.minimum(closest_dist_sq, distance_to_candidates, out=distance_to_candidates)\n        candidates_pot = distance_to_candidates @ sample_weight.reshape(-1, 1)\n\n        # Decide which candidate is the best\n        best_candidate = np.argmin(candidates_pot)\n        current_pot = candidates_pot[best_candidate]\n        closest_dist_sq = distance_to_candidates[best_candidate]\n        best_candidate = candidate_ids[best_candidate]\n\n        # Permanently add best center candidate found in local tries\n        if sp.issparse(X):\n            centers[c] = X[[best_candidate]].toarray()\n        else:\n            centers[c] = X[best_candidate]\n        indices[c] = best_candidate\n\n    return centers, indices\n\n\n###############################################################################\n# K-means batch estimation by EM (expectation maximization)\n\n\ndef _tolerance(X, tol):\n    \"\"\"Return a tolerance which is dependent on the dataset.\"\"\"\n    if tol == 0:\n        return 0\n    if sp.issparse(X):\n        variances = mean_variance_axis(X, axis=0)[1]\n    else:\n        variances = np.var(X, axis=0)\n    return np.mean(variances) * tol\n\n\n@validate_params(\n    {\n        \"X\": [\"array-like\", \"sparse matrix\"],\n        \"sample_weight\": [\"array-like\", None],\n        \"return_n_iter\": [bool],\n    },\n    prefer_skip_nested_validation=False,\n)\ndef k_means(\n    X,\n    n_clusters,\n    *,\n    sample_weight=None,\n    init=\"k-means++\",\n    n_init=\"auto\",\n    max_iter=300,\n    verbose=False,\n    tol=1e-4,\n    random_state=None,\n    copy_x=True,\n    algorithm=\"lloyd\",\n    return_n_iter=False,\n):\n    \"\"\"Perform K-means clustering algorithm.\n\n    Read more in the :ref:`User Guide <k_means>`.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    X : {array-like, sparse matrix} of shape (n_samples, n_features)\n        The observations to cluster. It must be noted that the data\n        will be converted to C ordering, which will cause a memory copy\n        if the given data is not C-contiguous.\n\n    n_clusters : int\n        The number of clusters to form as well as the number of\n        centroids to generate.\n\n    sample_weight : array-like of shape (n_samples,), default=None\n        The weights for each observation in `X`. If `None`, all observations\n        are assigned equal weight. `sample_weight` is not used during\n        initialization if `init` is a callable or a user provided array.\n\n    init : {'k-means++', 'random'}, callable or array-like of shape \\\n            (n_clusters, n_features), default='k-means++'\n        Method for initialization:\n\n        - `'k-means++'` : selects initial cluster centers for k-mean\n          clustering in a smart way to speed up convergence. See section\n          Notes in k_init for more details.\n        - `'random'`: choose `n_clusters` observations (rows) at random from data\n          for the initial centroids.\n        - If an array is passed, it should be of shape `(n_clusters, n_features)`\n          and gives the initial centers.\n        - If a callable is passed, it should take arguments `X`, `n_clusters` and a\n          random state and return an initialization.\n\n    n_init : 'auto' or int, default=\"auto\"\n        Number of time the k-means algorithm will be run with different\n        centroid seeds. The final results will be the best output of\n        n_init consecutive runs in terms of inertia.\n\n        When `n_init='auto'`, the number of runs depends on the value of init:\n        10 if using `init='random'` or `init` is a callable;\n        1 if using `init='k-means++'` or `init` is an array-like.\n\n        .. versionadded:: 1.2\n           Added 'auto' option for `n_init`.\n\n        .. versionchanged:: 1.4\n           Default value for `n_init` changed to `'auto'`.\n\n    max_iter : int, default=300\n        Maximum number of iterations of the k-means algorithm to run.\n\n    verbose : bool, default=False\n        Verbosity mode.\n\n    tol : float, default=1e-4\n        Relative tolerance with regards to Frobenius norm of the difference\n        in the cluster centers of two consecutive iterations to declare\n        convergence.\n\n    random_state : int, RandomState instance or None, default=None\n        Determines random number generation for centroid initialization. Use\n        an int to make the randomness deterministic.\n        See :term:`Glossary <random_state>`.\n\n    copy_x : bool, default=True\n        When pre-computing distances it is more numerically accurate to center\n        the data first. If `copy_x` is True (default), then the original data is\n        not modified. If False, the original data is modified, and put back\n        before the function returns, but small numerical differences may be\n        introduced by subtracting and then adding the data mean. Note that if\n        the original data is not C-contiguous, a copy will be made even if\n        `copy_x` is False. If the original data is sparse, but not in CSR format,\n        a copy will be made even if `copy_x` is False.\n\n    algorithm : {\"lloyd\", \"elkan\"}, default=\"lloyd\"\n        K-means algorithm to use. The classical EM-style algorithm is `\"lloyd\"`.\n        The `\"elkan\"` variation can be more efficient on some datasets with\n        well-defined clusters, by using the triangle inequality. However it's\n        more memory intensive due to the allocation of an extra array of shape\n        `(n_samples, n_clusters)`.\n\n        .. versionchanged:: 0.18\n            Added Elkan algorithm\n\n        .. versionchanged:: 1.1\n            Renamed \"full\" to \"lloyd\", and deprecated \"auto\" and \"full\".\n            Changed \"auto\" to use \"lloyd\" instead of \"elkan\".\n\n    return_n_iter : bool, default=False\n        Whether or not to return the number of iterations.\n\n    Returns\n    -------\n    centroid : ndarray of shape (n_clusters, n_features)\n        Centroids found at the last iteration of k-means.\n\n    label : ndarray of shape (n_samples,)\n        The `label[i]` is the code or index of the centroid the\n        i'th observation is closest to.\n\n    inertia : float\n        The final value of the inertia criterion (sum of squared distances to\n        the closest centroid for all observations in the training set).\n\n    best_n_iter : int\n        Number of iterations corresponding to the best results.\n        Returned only if `return_n_iter` is set to True.\n\n    Examples\n    --------\n    >>> import numpy as np\n    >>> from sklearn.cluster import k_means\n    >>> X = np.array([[1, 2], [1, 4], [1, 0],\n    ...               [10, 2], [10, 4], [10, 0]])\n    >>> centroid, label, inertia = k_means(\n    ...     X, n_clusters=2, n_init=\"auto\", random_state=0\n    ... )\n    >>> centroid\n    array([[10.,  2.],\n           [ 1.,  2.]])\n    >>> label\n    array([1, 1, 1, 0, 0, 0], dtype=int32)\n    >>> inertia\n    16.0\n    \"\"\"\n    est = KMeans(\n        n_clusters=n_clusters,\n        init=init,\n        n_init=n_init,\n        max_iter=max_iter,\n        verbose=verbose,\n        tol=tol,\n        random_state=random_state,\n        copy_x=copy_x,\n        algorithm=algorithm,\n    ).fit(X, sample_weight=sample_weight)\n    if return_n_iter:\n        return est.cluster_centers_, est.labels_, est.inertia_, est.n_iter_\n    else:\n        return est.cluster_centers_, est.labels_, est.inertia_\n\n\ndef _kmeans_single_elkan(\n    X,\n    sample_weight,\n    centers_init,\n    max_iter=300,\n    verbose=False,\n    tol=1e-4,\n    n_threads=1,\n):\n    \"\"\"A single run of k-means elkan, assumes preparation completed prior.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    X : {ndarray, sparse matrix} of shape (n_samples, n_features)\n        The observations to cluster. If sparse matrix, must be in CSR format.\n\n    sample_weight : array-like of shape (n_samples,)\n        The weights for each observation in X.\n\n    centers_init : ndarray of shape (n_clusters, n_features)\n        The initial centers.\n\n    max_iter : int, default=300\n        Maximum number of iterations of the k-means algorithm to run.\n\n    verbose : bool, default=False\n        Verbosity mode.\n\n    tol : float, default=1e-4\n        Relative tolerance with regards to Frobenius norm of the difference\n        in the cluster centers of two consecutive iterations to declare\n        convergence.\n        It's not advised to set `tol=0` since convergence might never be\n        declared due to rounding errors. Use a very small number instead.\n\n    n_threads : int, default=1\n        The number of OpenMP threads to use for the computation. Parallelism is\n        sample-wise on the main cython loop which assigns each sample to its\n        closest center.\n\n    Returns\n    -------\n    centroid : ndarray of shape (n_clusters, n_features)\n        Centroids found at the last iteration of k-means.\n\n    label : ndarray of shape (n_samples,)\n        label[i] is the code or index of the centroid the\n        i'th observation is closest to.\n\n    inertia : float\n        The final value of the inertia criterion (sum of squared distances to\n        the closest centroid for all observations in the training set).\n\n    n_iter : int\n        Number of iterations run.\n    \"\"\"\n    n_samples = X.shape[0]\n    n_clusters = centers_init.shape[0]\n\n    # Buffers to avoid new allocations at each iteration.\n    centers = centers_init\n    centers_new = np.zeros_like(centers)\n    weight_in_clusters = np.zeros(n_clusters, dtype=X.dtype)\n    labels = np.full(n_samples, -1, dtype=np.int32)\n    labels_old = labels.copy()\n    center_half_distances = euclidean_distances(centers) / 2\n    distance_next_center = np.partition(\n        np.asarray(center_half_distances), kth=1, axis=0\n    )[1]\n    upper_bounds = np.zeros(n_samples, dtype=X.dtype)\n    lower_bounds = np.zeros((n_samples, n_clusters), dtype=X.dtype)\n    center_shift = np.zeros(n_clusters, dtype=X.dtype)\n\n    if sp.issparse(X):\n        init_bounds = init_bounds_sparse\n        elkan_iter = elkan_iter_chunked_sparse\n        _inertia = _inertia_sparse\n    else:\n        init_bounds = init_bounds_dense\n        elkan_iter = elkan_iter_chunked_dense\n        _inertia = _inertia_dense\n\n    init_bounds(\n        X,\n        centers,\n        center_half_distances,\n        labels,\n        upper_bounds,\n        lower_bounds,\n        n_threads=n_threads,\n    )\n\n    strict_convergence = False\n\n    for i in range(max_iter):\n        elkan_iter(\n            X,\n            sample_weight,\n            centers,\n            centers_new,\n            weight_in_clusters,\n            center_half_distances,\n            distance_next_center,\n            upper_bounds,\n            lower_bounds,\n            labels,\n            center_shift,\n            n_threads,\n        )\n\n        # compute new pairwise distances between centers and closest other\n        # center of each center for next iterations\n        center_half_distances = euclidean_distances(centers_new) / 2\n        distance_next_center = np.partition(\n            np.asarray(center_half_distances), kth=1, axis=0\n        )[1]\n\n        if verbose:\n            inertia = _inertia(X, sample_weight, centers, labels, n_threads)\n            print(f\"Iteration {i}, inertia {inertia}\")\n\n        centers, centers_new = centers_new, centers\n\n        if np.array_equal(labels, labels_old):\n            # First check the labels for strict convergence.\n            if verbose:\n                print(f\"Converged at iteration {i}: strict convergence.\")\n            strict_convergence = True\n            break\n        else:\n            # No strict convergence, check for tol based convergence.\n            center_shift_tot = (center_shift**2).sum()\n            if center_shift_tot <= tol:\n                if verbose:\n                    print(\n                        f\"Converged at iteration {i}: center shift \"\n                        f\"{center_shift_tot} within tolerance {tol}.\"\n                    )\n                break\n\n        labels_old[:] = labels\n\n    if not strict_convergence:\n        # rerun E-step so that predicted labels match cluster centers\n        elkan_iter(\n            X,\n            sample_weight,\n            centers,\n            centers,\n            weight_in_clusters,\n            center_half_distances,\n            distance_next_center,\n            upper_bounds,\n            lower_bounds,\n            labels,\n            center_shift,\n            n_threads,\n            update_centers=False,\n        )\n\n    inertia = _inertia(X, sample_weight, centers, labels, n_threads)\n\n    return labels, inertia, centers, i + 1\n\n\n# Threadpoolctl context to limit the number of threads in second level of\n# nested parallelism (i.e. BLAS) to avoid oversubscription.\n@_threadpool_controller_decorator(limits=1, user_api=\"blas\")\ndef _kmeans_single_lloyd(\n    X,\n    sample_weight,\n    centers_init,\n    max_iter=300,\n    verbose=False,\n    tol=1e-4,\n    n_threads=1,\n):\n    \"\"\"A single run of k-means lloyd, assumes preparation completed prior.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    X : {ndarray, sparse matrix} of shape (n_samples, n_features)\n        The observations to cluster. If sparse matrix, must be in CSR format.\n\n    sample_weight : ndarray of shape (n_samples,)\n        The weights for each observation in X.\n\n    centers_init : ndarray of shape (n_clusters, n_features)\n        The initial centers.\n\n    max_iter : int, default=300\n        Maximum number of iterations of the k-means algorithm to run.\n\n    verbose : bool, default=False\n        Verbosity mode\n\n    tol : float, default=1e-4\n        Relative tolerance with regards to Frobenius norm of the difference\n        in the cluster centers of two consecutive iterations to declare\n        convergence.\n        It's not advised to set `tol=0` since convergence might never be\n        declared due to rounding errors. Use a very small number instead.\n\n    n_threads : int, default=1\n        The number of OpenMP threads to use for the computation. Parallelism is\n        sample-wise on the main cython loop which assigns each sample to its\n        closest center.\n\n    Returns\n    -------\n    centroid : ndarray of shape (n_clusters, n_features)\n        Centroids found at the last iteration of k-means.\n\n    label : ndarray of shape (n_samples,)\n        label[i] is the code or index of the centroid the\n        i'th observation is closest to.\n\n    inertia : float\n        The final value of the inertia criterion (sum of squared distances to\n        the closest centroid for all observations in the training set).\n\n    n_iter : int\n        Number of iterations run.\n    \"\"\"\n    n_clusters = centers_init.shape[0]\n\n    # Buffers to avoid new allocations at each iteration.\n    centers = centers_init\n    centers_new = np.zeros_like(centers)\n    labels = np.full(X.shape[0], -1, dtype=np.int32)\n    labels_old = labels.copy()\n    weight_in_clusters = np.zeros(n_clusters, dtype=X.dtype)\n    center_shift = np.zeros(n_clusters, dtype=X.dtype)\n\n    if sp.issparse(X):\n        lloyd_iter = lloyd_iter_chunked_sparse\n        _inertia = _inertia_sparse\n    else:\n        lloyd_iter = lloyd_iter_chunked_dense\n        _inertia = _inertia_dense\n\n    strict_convergence = False\n\n    for i in range(max_iter):\n        lloyd_iter(\n            X,\n            sample_weight,\n            centers,\n            centers_new,\n            weight_in_clusters,\n            labels,\n            center_shift,\n            n_threads,\n        )\n\n        if verbose:\n            inertia = _inertia(X, sample_weight, centers, labels, n_threads)\n            print(f\"Iteration {i}, inertia {inertia}.\")\n\n        centers, centers_new = centers_new, centers\n\n        if np.array_equal(labels, labels_old):\n            # rerun E-step so that predicted labels match cluster centers\n        lloyd_iter(\n            X,\n            sample_weight,\n            centers,\n            centers,\n            weight_in_clusters,\n            labels,\n            center_shift,\n            n_threads,\n            update_centers=False,\n        )\n\n    inertia = _inertia(X, sample_weight, centers, labels, n_threads)\n\n    return labels, inertia, centers, i + 1\n\n\ndef _labels_inertia(X, sample_weight, centers, n_threads=1, return_inertia=True):\n    \"\"\"E step of the K-means EM algorithm.\n\n    Compute the labels and the inertia of the given samples and centers.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    X : {ndarray, sparse matrix} of shape (n_samples, n_features)\n        The input samples to assign to the labels.     _labels = lloyd_iter_chunked_dense\n        _inertia = _inertia_dense\n\n    _labels(\n        X,\n        sample_weight,\n        centers,\n        centers_new=None,\n
